# Unsloth SPDX and copyleft-import scan (Phase 0.4)

Generated by a scan of two shallow clones. Per-file detail is in `docs/_audit/unsloth-spdx.csv` (one row per source file).

| repo | commit | source files scanned |
|---|---|---|
| unsloth | `688f381b0c3328e32b6ab3593f0e192c3be73876` | 10228 |
| unsloth-zoo | `dcc56b55295b617397bc815a99ce971490bb7694` | 1373 |

## Method

- Source files: extensions .py .pyi .js .mjs .cjs .jsx .ts .tsx .sh .ps1 .c .cc .cpp .cu .cuh .h .hpp .rs .go, all directories except `.git`, `node_modules`, `__pycache__`.
- SPDX: `SPDX-License-Identifier` in the first 30 lines. Text markers (GNU Affero, GNU Lesser, Apache License, MIT, BSD) in the same window are in the CSV column `header_text_markers`.
- Effective license: the SPDX header if present, otherwise the nearest directory license file (`LICENSE*` preferred over `COPYING`). Family is AGPL / LGPL / GPL / Apache / MIT / BSD / Other. An alternative such as `Apache-2.0 OR LGPL` counts as copyleft (conservative).
- Import graph: Python `import` / `from ... import` from the AST (including imports inside functions), resolved within both repos (absolute names are searched from the importing directory upward, then the repo roots). Relative imports are resolved from the file. JS/TS relative imports are resolved; aliased (`@/`) imports are not, so the frontend graph is incomplete. Non-Python/JS files have no import graph (`n/a`).
- Copyleft target: effective family AGPL, LGPL or GPL, or any file under `studio/` or `unsloth_cli/`.
- `imports_copyleft` = a copyleft target is reachable from the file through static internal imports (transitively, across both repos). `copyleft_via_runtime_only` = reachable only if the parent package `__init__.py` files that Python executes on import are counted.

| repo | Apache | MIT | BSD | AGPL | LGPL | GPL | Other | total |

| unsloth-zoo | 2 | 73 | 0 | 153 | 1145 | 0 | 0 | 1373 |
| **both** | 411 | 92 | 0 | 9953 | 1145 | 0 | 0 | **11601** |

Raw effective values (top):

- unsloth: `AGPL-3.0-only` 9657, `Apache-2.0` 409, `AGPL-3.0` 119, `MIT` 19, `GNU Affero General Public License v3.0` 13, `AGPL-3.0-or-later` 11
- unsloth-zoo: `LGPL-3.0` 1143, `AGPL-3.0-only` 146, `MIT` 73, `AGPL-3.0-or-later` 7, `LGPL-3.0-or-later` 2, `Apache-2.0` 2

## Header conflicts in the core package

The repo root `LICENSE` is Apache-2.0 and the README says the core package is Apache-2.0, but per-file headers disagree in many places. Under the header-first rule:

- `unsloth/` core package source files: 212; effective Apache 77, effective AGPL 135, other 0.
- Files whose header family differs from their directory license family: 2304 (repo-wide). Breakdown (directory family -> header family): Apache -> AGPL 2150, LGPL -> AGPL 153, AGPL -> Apache 1.
- Action for the fork: treat header-AGPL core files as AGPL until the upstream maintainers confirm; do not rely on the root LICENSE alone.

## Packaging

- `unsloth` wheel (`unsloth/pyproject.toml`, `[tool.setuptools.packages.find] include = ["unsloth*", "unsloth_cli*", "studio", "studio.backend*"]`, `include-package-data = true`, `license = "Apache-2.0"`): packages the Apache `unsloth` package together with the AGPL `unsloth_cli` and `studio` trees. Python files that match the include list, before the exclude list: AGPL 3663, Apache 78, MIT 17. A wheel built from this tree is therefore not Apache-only.
- `unsloth-zoo` wheel (`unsloth-zoo/pyproject.toml`, `license = "LGPL-3.0-or-later"`): `unsloth_zoo`, `unsloth_zoo.*`, `unsloth_zoo_downloads`, `unsloth_zoo_downloads.*`. Python files in the wheel before its exclude list: LGPL 210, MIT 73, Apache 2, AGPL 1.
- `unsloth` core dependencies (`[project] dependencies`) do not list `unsloth-zoo`. It appears only in optional extras. Core code references it in 72 files. The core `__init__.py` imports it on the MLX path and in the dataset helpers (inside try or if blocks), and `_cpu_init.py` imports `unsloth_zoo.cpu` at module level on the CPU path. So it is not an install-time requirement, but core needs it at runtime on those paths.

## Notes and limits

- Static analysis only. Dynamic imports (`importlib` with computed names), `subprocess` launches of `studio/` or `unsloth_cli`, and aliased JS imports are not followed.
- Short module names (for example `utils`) are resolved to the nearest directory that contains them, which can add false edges. The CSV lists every edge so it can be checked.
- The header-first rule follows the phase spec. The directory-license view would classify the core package as Apache; the difference is the header-conflict section above.
